*Idea 5 scores on a different axis (storage venue/reviewers); it is the pivot, not the primary.

Idea 4 — Tail-aware CXL object routing (condensed)

Title. "Taming the Microsecond Tail: Latency-Predictable Placement on Real CXL Memory." Thesis. Per-device tail telemetry can steer latency-critical objects away from tail-prone devices/moments, converting Melody's observations into mitigation. Open because. Melody documents µs tails "even under low device bandwidth utilization" and punts mitigation; LiteSwitch (OSDI'26, embargoed/low-conf) tolerates stalls rather than avoiding them. Design. Canary probes + PEBS percentile tracker feeding an ObjPlace routing rule. Risk (decisive). Hostage to CZ120 behavior — Melody saw tails on some devices; if CZ120s are tail-stable there is no paper (hardware-quirk dependence). Objection/defense. "Vendor anomaly, not a system problem" — only survivable if tails reproduce across load mixes and devices. Verdict (inference). Not a standalone bet: it is Week-2's experiment and Idea 1's third placement signal.

Idea 5 — famfs three-tier buffer/storage system (condensed)

Title. "A Storage-Semantics Middle Tier on Production CXL Silicon." Thesis. CXL is a viable DRAM/SSD middle tier for buffer pools when managed with storage-aware admission/eviction, validated for the first time on production ASICs. Open because (fact). The only real-HW validation is a workshop paper on one Seagate FPGA (HardBD&Active'24) whose authors name scalability and device-sensitivity as future work; famfs disclaims storage semantics and is unmerged. Hardware. famfs already deployed on 2 CZ120s — rare. Risks. famfs churn (standalone→FUSE), volatility (scope to caching only), DB-engineering effort. Venue. FAST'28. Verdict. The sanctioned pivot if de-interleaving fails (session-10 decision), not the primary.

Idea 6 — DB operators under CXL contention (condensed)

Extend Weisgut (VLDB'25) — static operator placement on 4 FPGA blades — to concurrent queries with contention on commodity ASICs. Weakness (inference). Competes on the DB community's turf with heavy engine engineering; the novel ingredient (contention) is better spent in Ideas 1/2; venue would be VLDB, off the target list. Keep as a workload for Idea 2, not a paper.

3. Deep dive on top 3

3.1 Idea 1 — "One Node Is Not Enough: Device-Granular Memory Management for Direct-Attached CXL"

Problem. Linux and every published system collapse direct-attached CXL into one CPU-less NUMA node (or one per region), erasing device identity: per-device bandwidth ceilings (~35.5 GiB/s on CZ120; corroborated by Melody's 22–52 GB/s device range and ADMS'25's 43 GB/s), per-device queueing/tails, and the option to isolate on disjoint devices. No system decides which CXL device data lives on; kernels decide only which tier (TPP→PACT'26 lineage — fact).

Motivating example. Embedding-serving (bandwidth-bound gathers) co-located with a graph process (latency-tolerant, cold-heavy). Pooled: both stripe across the fused node; gathers contend — the measured 2.0–2.13× victim slowdown. Device-granular: embedding gets devices {0,1} striped 2-wide; graph capacity lands on {2,3}; contention vanishes without throttling.

Key observation (inference; to prove in Weeks 1–2). A fused pool gives the average of N devices; independent devices give N choices. When per-device bandwidth saturates at 4 threads, choice beats averaging whenever demand is skewed, phased, or adversarial.

System design. (a) Device-state monitor: per-device bandwidth estimate, queue-pressure proxy (loaded-latency canaries), tail tracker. (b) Planner: extends ObjPlace's benefit-per-byte planner from {DRAM, CXL} to {DRAM, dev0..devN, stripe-sets}; candidates = single-device pinning, k-wide striping, DRAM, chosen by object sensitivity class × per-device pressure. (c) Bounded migrator: object-granular movement between devices and back to DRAM on pressure/phase shifts, rate-limited (the 2026-06-12 "movement" component; device-awareness keeps it out of the page-hotness lane).

Policy. Score(o, t) = predicted-benefit(o.sensitivity, t.headroom & tail state) − migration-cost(o.size); greedy under per-device capacity + DRAM budget; pressure hysteresis against thrash; INTERLEAVE candidate = stripe across k least-loaded devices (fixes the GNN-uniform −11.6% hole).

Measure first. (i) De-interleaved per-device baselines (are identical CZ120s identical?); (ii) the decisive cross-device interference matrix (victim dev-A, aggressor dev-B, disjoint cores); (iii) per-device tails under load; (iv) pooled-vs-partitioned aggregate bandwidth under skewed demand.

Prototype. ObjPlace extension (system/placement/): multi-node registry, telemetry thread, planner candidates, move_pages()-based object migration. User-space only; admin needed once for decoder reconfiguration.

Experiments. E1 interference matrix (the never-published figure); E2 pooled vs device-aware, single multi-phase app; E3 two/three real tenants (embedding server + graph + batch), device-aware vs pooled vs static partition; E4 signal ablation (sensitivity/pressure/tail); E5 baseline sweep; E6 2-device vs 4-device scaling (defends "gains from mechanism, not hardware").

Figures/tables. F1 per-device saturation + interference heatmap; F2 pooled-vs-partitioned bandwidth under skew; F3 architecture; F4 end-to-end tenant throughput/p99 vs baselines; F5 ablation; F6 migration timeline under phase change; T1 related-work matrix (device-aware × real-ASIC × multi-tenant); T2 testbed; T3 overheads.

Main claims. C1 first measurement of CXL-to-CXL cross-device interference + per-device behavior of identical commodity ASICs on one host; C2 fusing devices sacrifices X% isolatable bandwidth / Y× tail; C3 a device-granular runtime recovers most of it online, beating pooled + best-static by Z%; C4 device-awareness is orthogonal to tier-hotness systems.

Abstract (draft). "Operating systems expose direct-attached CXL memory as a single CPU-less NUMA node, fusing multiple physical devices into one undifferentiated pool. Using four identical CZ120 CXL expanders on one host, we present the first characterization of what this fusion hides: per-device bandwidth ceilings that saturate at four threads, cross-device interference asymmetries, and microsecond tail divergence between nominally identical devices. We then present [Name], a runtime that treats each CXL device as an independently schedulable resource, placing and migrating application objects across DRAM and individual devices using per-device pressure and tail telemetry plus object sensitivity. On multi-tenant workloads, [Name] improves aggregate throughput by ...% and reduces p99 latency by ...× over pooled and statically partitioned configurations, while matching the best static placement within ...% on stable workloads."

Intro story. P1: CXL is deployed and fleets attach several expanders per host (Micron 8-dev, Memstrata 3-card, our 4-dev). P2: software sees one node — the map's device treatments (26 single / 10 pooled / 7 independent-but-unmanaged) prove nobody manages them. P3: measurement teaser (matrix + pooling cost). P4: system + contributions.

Eval structure. 6.1 testbed/methodology; 6.2 characterization; 6.3 single-app gains; 6.4 multi-tenant; 6.5 ablation/overheads; 6.6 device-count sensitivity (2 vs 4); 6.7 baselines incl. tier-hotness systems.

Strongest related-work comparison. Memstrata (OSDI'24): multi-tenant on real CXL but 3 cards hardware-fused, VM-granular, colors above the pool — we operate below the pool abstraction. Melody (ASPLOS'25): measures 4 devices on different hosts, no management. Weisgut (VLDB'25): 4 independent nodes but static, DB-only, FPGA, no contention. Colloid (SOSP'24): nearest policy relative (balance by loaded latency) — page-level, emulated, two tiers; we generalize to N devices with object semantics. SoarAlto (OSDI'25)/OBASE (OSDI'26)/Clove (arXiv'26): object-level but device-blind.

Most dangerous criticism. "This is just NUMA — multi-node placement is 30 years old." Secondary: "identical devices on independent links shouldn't interfere; if they don't, the premise dies."

Neutralizing evidence. Show CXL nodes violate classical NUMA assumptions: CPU-less nodes make local-vs-remote meaningless; ~9× lower per-device ceiling saturating at 4 threads (DRAM saturates far later); tails absent on DRAM; AutoNUMA/weighted-interleave measurably mishandle them (run as baselines; ADMS'25 already shows prefetcher/interleave pathologies). If cross-device interference is nil: that finding itself redirects to Idea 2's partitioning argument (choice/isolation still beats averaging under skew) — Week 2 resolves this before commitment.

Venue. OSDI'27 or ASPLOS'27 (deadlines ~Dec 2026). Difficulty. High-medium (ObjPlace extension, not a from-scratch build). Publishability. Highest of all options (inference). Prior work. Occupies the verified empty cell; must cite CSFQ, Weisgut, CXL-CCL, Liaw&Chen, Equilibria, MTTM, Melody, CXL-Interplay.

3.2 Idea 2 — "Devices as Isolation Domains: Multi-Tenant Bandwidth Isolation on Commodity CXL"

Problem. All multi-tenant CXL systems share one pool and regulate temporally: Memstrata colors pages and reallocates DRAM (OSDI'24); MTTM partitions fast-tier bandwidth (EuroSys'26, emulated); Equilibria enforces fairness on one node (arXiv'26); CXL-Interplay proposes device-side MBA-like throttling as future work (DAC'25). Temporal regulation taxes someone. With N devices, spatial isolation is free: tenants on disjoint devices.

Motivating example. Our measurement is the motivation: a bandwidth-bound CXL victim suffers 2.0–2.13× under a same-region co-runner while DRAM co-location costs ~nothing. Victim and aggressor on different devices should collapse to ~1× (to show). Same-device 2.1× vs cross-device ~1.0× is the thesis in one figure.

Key observation. The device is the natural isolation quantum for CXL: bandwidth queues are per-device, so device-granular partitioning removes contention at the source instead of policing it after the fact. Fragmentation is the price; a scheduler manages it (harvest idle devices; share only between compatible tenants).

Design. Tenant-level scheduler above per-device NUMA nodes: admission assigns each tenant a device set (sized by bandwidth demand + capacity); compatibility matrix (bandwidth-bound tenants never share; latency-tolerant may); harvester lends idle devices to bursty tenants with bounded-migration reclamation; enforcement via cgroups/cpusets + mempolicy (no kernel changes).

Policy. Bin-pack tenants→devices under capacity + bandwidth-class constraints, minimizing expected interference (predicted from the measured pairwise matrix); online re-packing on SLO violation.

Measure first. Idea 1's matrix + pairwise tenant-class contention (BW×BW, BW×latency, latency×latency) same- vs cross-device; reclamation cost of a harvested device.

Prototype. Tenant-manager daemon + numactl/mempolicy enforcement (+ ObjPlace inside tenants optionally). Fastest path to a complete paper (inference).

Experiments. E1 isolation bar chart (money figure); E2 2–4 real tenants under fused pool vs static partition vs dynamic partition+harvest; E3 vs reimplemented Memstrata-style coloring and MTTM-style throttling (stated explicitly as reimplementations); E4 harvest/reclaim under bursty arrivals (BurstGPT/Azure-LLM trace shapes); E5 fragmentation/stranding analysis.

Figures. Isolation bars; SLO-violation timelines under bursts; throughput-vs-fairness Pareto (spatial vs temporal regulation); reclamation-latency CDF; stranding table.

Claims. First spatial (device-level) isolation mechanism for CXL multi-tenancy; near-perfect bandwidth isolation at zero throttling cost; quantified fragmentation trade-off; harvesting recovers stranded capacity.

Abstract (sketch). "...on hosts with multiple CXL expanders, the device is the natural isolation domain: assigning tenants to disjoint devices eliminates the 2.1× bandwidth interference we measure on shared devices, without throttling. [Name] schedules tenants across four CZ120 devices, harvesting idle capacity..."

Intro story. Cloud CXL is multi-tenant (Pond, Memstrata); regulation-above-a-pool is the only tool today; we add the missing spatial tool and show when each wins.

Eval structure. Characterize → isolate → schedule → vs temporal regulation → harvest dynamics → costs.

Strongest comparison. Memstrata: its 3 devices are hardware-fused by Flat Memory Mode — it literally cannot express "tenant A on device 0". MTTM partitions the DRAM tier and is NUMA-emulated (0 devices — fact). CSFQ (NSDI'26): per-channel QoS but in switch transport for multi-host pools — different layer and topology.

Most dangerous criticism. "Static partitioning is trivial — numactl can do this" and "fragmentation makes it impractical; pooling exists to avoid stranding."

Neutralization. The research is (a) the first quantification of what sharing costs on real devices, (b) the dynamic part — harvesting + compatibility-aware sharing + bounded reclamation, which numactl cannot do, (c) the head-to-head vs temporal regulation producing a Pareto frontier. Address stranding with the harvester and quantify stranding-vs-isolation explicitly (Pond's stranding framing, which reviewers know).

Venue. OSDI/SOSP/EuroSys. Difficulty. Medium (least new code). Publishability. High; cleanest story (inference).

3.3 Idea 3 — "Beyond Static Weights: Workload-Adaptive Interleaving Across DRAM and Multiple CXL Devices"

Problem. Linux weighted interleave (kernel 6.9+) sets static, global, per-node weights. ADMS'25 (fact): no static DRAM:CXL ratio is optimal — optima shift up to 88% with operator parameters, prefetchers interact pathologically, and the adaptive runtime is left unbuilt. Micron/Intel (fact): optimal ratios flip with R/W mix (DRAM loses ~20% at 1:1 while CXL improves). With N devices there is a second unexplored knob: stripe width (1-/2-/4-wide) trading peak bandwidth against blast radius and queueing.

Motivating example. Our GNN result: uniform access lost 11.6% because ObjPlace lacked an INTERLEAVE candidate; skewed access won +14.3% because concentration beat striping. No static ratio or width serves both phases of one application.

Key observation (inference; static evidence from ADMS'25 + Micron). Interleave ratio and width are per-object-class, per-phase decisions computable online from sensitivity + R/W mix + device load.

Design. Planner emits {ratio, width, device-set} per object class; user-space striping via per-object mbind across chosen nodes; PEBS R/W-mix monitor retunes ratios; phase detector triggers bounded re-striping.

Method. Measure ratio×width×threads×R/W response surfaces (heatmaps); workloads: embedding gather, GNN, membench mixes, SSB-style scan/join kernels (meet ADMS'25 on its ground); metrics: throughput, loaded latency, achieved-vs-oracle gap; baselines: DRAM-only, oracle-tuned best-static weighted interleave (strong baseline), Linux default, ObjPlace-without-INTERLEAVE.

Claims. First adaptive interleaving runtime on real multi-device CXL; within ε of per-workload oracle while no static config is; width matters independently of ratio (new axis).

Comparison. ADMS'25 (proposed, never built), Micron (static, fused), HMSDK/kernel weighted interleave (static), Beluga (fixed 2 MB stripe, hint-free, switch pool — SIGMOD'26).

Most dangerous criticism. "Parameter auto-tuning, not a system"; overlap with Idea 1 could read as one paper.

Defense / honest recommendation (inference). Position width selection + phase-driven re-striping + object granularity as mechanisms with measured migration costs; show cases where no static point is within 20%+ of adaptive. Default: make this the steering-mechanism section of Idea 1 rather than a standalone submission, unless response surfaces are dramatic.

Venue. ASPLOS/EuroSys. Difficulty. Medium. Publishability. Medium-high standalone; high as a chapter of Idea 1.

4. Weak or risky ideas to avoid

  • Pure multi-device characterization ("Melody-for-CZ120s"). Benchmark study without a system; Melody exists. Use as Section 2 of Ideas 1/2.
  • R/W-mix steering alone. Micron published the phenomenon; it is one signal inside Idea 3.
  • Per-device power/thermal/tail correlation. Novel datum, no compelling workload, quirk-dependent. A paragraph, not a paper.
  • Kernel-level device-aware tiering. Needs root/kernel on a shared cluster node; lands in the TPP→PACT'26 crowd. User space is the right vehicle.
  • KV-cache-on-CXL serving tier. Own layer-split null result; Beluga (SIGMOD'26) and TraCT own the lane. Revisit only with request/recency-skew redesign.
  • Object-level sensitivity tiering as the headline. SoarAlto (OSDI'25), OBASE (OSDI'26), Clove (arXiv'26) close that window.
  • Switch-pool / multi-host anything. No switch hardware; OSDI'26 is saturating it (RamRyder, Blowfish, MDK, Duhu, MEGALON...).
  • GPF/crash-consistency. CZ120s are volatile; cannot exercise end-to-end.
  • Anything whose result reads "farther memory is slower". Reframe around what NUMA machinery gets wrong for CXL, or cut.

5. Recommended first experiments (before any new system code)

  1. The gate: admin request to tear down region decoder0.2 and recreate at interleave_ways=1 → 2 per-device NUMA nodes from dax2.0 (famfs devices untouched, per session-10). Verify: cxl list -RT, numactl -H.
  2. Per-device twins: membench latency/BW/saturation per device — are identical CZ120s identical in practice?
  3. The decisive matrix: victim on node A, aggressor on node B, disjoint cores — cross-device slowdown vs the same-device 2.0–2.13×.
  4. Tail scan: loaded-latency percentile sweeps per device (Melody-style).
  5. Pooling-loss demo: skewed two-app demand, fused vs split.

7. Suggested paper framing

Lead with the measurement nobody has (interference matrix / pooling-loss on identical commodity ASICs), then the abstraction argument (the device, not the node, is the schedulable unit), then the runtime (ObjPlace-extended placement + movement + tenant isolation), evaluated against both pool-based QoS (Memstrata/MTTM-style) and tier-hotness (AutoNUMA / oracle-static weighted interleave) baselines. Do NOT frame as: object-level tiering (SoarAlto/OBASE/Clove own it), hotness improvement (TPP lane), or characterization-first (Melody owns it). One paper = Idea 1 ∪ Idea 2's isolation results ∪ Idea 3's steering mechanism — exactly the combined thesis locked 2026-06-12, now on a 97-record evidence base with a named must-cite ring (CSFQ NSDI'26, Weisgut VLDB'25, CXL-CCL, Equilibria, MTTM, Melody, CXL-Interplay, Memstrata).

8. Final recommendation

Proceed with Idea 1 as the umbrella, scoped so Week-2 data picks the emphasis (interference-driven placement vs isolation-driven scheduling — both publishable), with Idea 3 folded in as the steering mechanism and Idea 5 (famfs/FAST) as the explicit pivot if the decoder gate fails. The admin ticket is the single critical-path item — file it first; everything else is under local control.
